- **p014-i8** · answer_option · structure · both · exam high
  - b. Epidermis
    - what: What does epidermis mean in this plant question? → It is the closely fitted outer cell covering that separates underlying plant tissues from the environment. _(outside the book)_
    - where: Where is its waxy secretion especially relevant? → On exposed aerial surfaces such as leaves and young stems, epidermal cells produce materials forming the cuticle. _(outside the book)_
      - why: Why does a plant need this barrier? → Exposed surfaces lose water to air. A water-resistant cuticle helps conserve water while the plant remains exposed to sunlight and air. _(outside the book)_
      - difference: How do epidermis and cuticle differ? → Epidermis is the cell layer; cuticle is the protective non-cellular covering deposited on its outside. A thicker cuticle does not mean the epidermis is generally the thickest tissue. _(outside the book)_
  - **khola (unpack):**

    - waxy substance yaani मोम जैसा पदार्थ जो सतह से पानी निकलना कम करे · image: मोम लगी सतह पर पानी की बूँद टिकना
    - kyon: खुली पत्ती से पानी हवा में निकल सकता है। _[standard]_ → Epidermis के बाहर की cuticle पानी का निकलना कम करती है। _[standard]_

- **p014-i11** · question · structure · both · exam high
  - Q. Site of gaseous exchange on leaf is:
    - what: What feature must the answer identify? → The question asks for the site on a leaf through which gases are exchanged.
    - where: Where does this exchange occur? → Primarily through stomatal pores in the leaf epidermis; option b is intended. _(outside the book)_
      - how: How do gases move through these pores? → Gases diffuse along concentration differences between the outside air and internal leaf air spaces. Carbon dioxide can enter for photosynthesis, while oxygen can leave. _(outside the book)_
      - why: Why is the distinction between stomata and guard cells important? → Stomatal pores provide the passage, while guard cells regulate its opening. A controller and the opening it controls are not the same structure. _(outside the book)_

    - aage: Changing pore size affects both gas exchange and water-vapour loss.
    - screen par: swell and open
    - is level ke liye: Separate opening, surrounding cells and regulatory function.
  - prerequisites: cells can gain or lose water; a pore is an opening
- **p014-i13** · answer_option · structure · both · exam high
  - b. Stomata
    - what: What are stomata? → They are tiny epidermal openings, each regulated by guard cells, that connect external air with internal leaf air spaces. _(outside the book)_
    - where: Where are they found on leaves? → They occur in the leaf epidermis. Many terrestrial broad leaves have more on the lower surface, but distribution varies. _(outside the book)_
      - why: Why must a protected leaf surface still have openings? → A protective covering limits water loss, but living leaf cells still need gas exchange for photosynthesis and respiration. Regulated pores help balance these needs. _(outside the book)_
  - **khola (unpack):**
    - Stomata yaani पत्ती की बाहरी परत के छोटे छेद, जिनसे gases अंदर-बाहर जाती हैं · image: बंद कमरे में हवा आने-जाने की खिड़की
    - kyon: Protective covering के बावजूद पत्ती को हवा से carbon dioxide चाहिए। _[standard]_ → Stomata बाहर की हवा और अंदर की खाली जगहों के बीच रास्ता देते हैं। _[standard]_
    - example / jagah: Microscopic openings in a leaf epidermis.
    - aage: Gas exchange also creates a route for water-vapour loss.
    - screen par: pass through
    - is level ke liye: Link epidermal protection with controlled exchange; stomata is plural, stoma singular.
  - prerequisites: leaf epidermis; photosynthesis uses carbon dioxide; diffusion
- **p014-i14** · answer_option · structure · both · exam high
  - c. Aerenchyma
    - what: What is aerenchyma? → It is specialised parenchyma with large air spaces that facilitate internal gas movement and can aid buoyancy in aquatic plants. _(outside the book)_
    - where: Where is it commonly associated with plant adaptation? → It is common in aquatic and waterlogged plants, where internal air spaces help gases reach tissues with limited access to air. _(outside the book)_
      - why: Why is it not the intended answer despite its connection with gases? → It provides internal air spaces, whereas this question asks for the leaf-surface exchange site, which is the stomatal pore. _(outside the book)_
  - **khola (unpack):**
    - Aerenchyma yaani बड़ी हवा वाली खाली जगहों वाला plant tissue · image: स्पंज के अंदर की खाली जगहें
    - kyon: पानी में डूबे हिस्सों तक बाहर की हवा आसानी से नहीं पहुँचती। _[standard]_ → बड़े अंदरूनी air spaces gases के आने-जाने में मदद करते हैं, पर ये सतह का छेद नहीं हैं। _[standard]_
    - example / jagah: Air-space tissue in aquatic plants such as water hyacinth.
    - aage: An air-related function alone does not identify the surface gas-exchange structure.
    - screen par: connect air spaces
    - is level ke liye: Remember the structural distinction: internal spaces versus epidermal pore.
  - prerequisites: parenchyma; spaces between cells; gases are needed by living plant cells
- **p014-i15** · answer_option · structure · both · exam high
  - d. Chlorenchyma
    - what: What is chlorenchyma? → It is parenchyma containing chloroplasts and specialised for photosynthesis. _(outside the book)_
    - where: Where is it commonly found? → It occurs in green plant parts, including photosynthetic tissue inside leaves. _(outside the book)_
      - why: Why is a photosynthetic tissue not the correct exchange-site answer? → It uses carbon dioxide after the gas reaches the tissue; the stomatal pore is the passage across the leaf surface. _(outside the book)_
  - **khola (unpack):**
    - Chlorenchyma yaani chloroplasts वाला plant tissue जो रोशनी से खाना बनाता है · image: हरी पत्ती के अंदर खाना बनाने की छोटी रसोई
    - kyon: पौधे को रोशनी की मदद से खाना बनाना है। _[standard]_ → Chloroplasts वाला tissue यह काम करता है; carbon dioxide के प्रवेश का रास्ता stomata हैं। _[standard]_
    - example / jagah: Photosynthetic tissue within a green leaf.
    - aage: Gas entry and gas use are related but distinct functions.
    - screen par: enter then use
    - is level ke liye: Contrast chlorenchyma–photosynthesis with aerenchyma–air spaces.
  - prerequisites: parenchyma; chloroplasts carry out photosynthesis
- **p014-i16** · question · structure · both · exam high
  - Q. Muscle is connected to bone via:
    - what: What connection is the question asking about? → It asks which structure joins muscle to bone.
    - where: Which structure supplies that attachment? → A tendon attaches muscle to bone; option b is intended. _(outside the book)_
      - how: How does this connection produce movement? → When a muscle contracts, its pull is transmitted through the tendon to the bone, helping move the bone at a joint. _(outside the book)_
      - difference: How is this different from a ligament's connection? → A tendon transmits muscle pull to bone; a ligament connects bone to bone and helps stabilise a joint. _(outside the book)_
  - prerequisites: muscles contract; bones meet at joints
- **p014-i17** · answer_option · structure · both · exam high
  - a. Ligament
    - what: What is a ligament? → It is a strong connective-tissue band joining bone to bone and helping stabilise a joint. _(outside the book)_
    - where: Where can this role be recognised? → Ligaments around the knee and ankle help hold the bones in appropriate relation during movement. _(outside the book)_
      - why: Why does a joint need ligaments rather than unrestricted movement? → Bones must move while remaining aligned; ligaments restrain excessive movement and help prevent instability. _(outside the book)_
  - **khola (unpack):**
    - Ligament yaani एक bone को दूसरी bone से जोड़कर joint को संभालने वाली मजबूत पट्टी · image: दो हिस्सों को साथ रखने वाला मजबूत strap
    - kyon: चलते समय joint की bones को अपनी जगह के संबंध में रहना चाहिए। _[standard]_ → Ligaments उन्हें जोड़कर जरूरत से ज्यादा खिसकना रोकने में मदद करते हैं। _[standard]_
    - example / jagah: Ligaments stabilising the knee or ankle.
    - aage: Ligament damage can make a joint unstable; this differs from losing a muscle's pulling connection.
    - screen par: strap together
    - is level ke liye: Match ligament to bone–bone and tendon to muscle–bone.
  - prerequisites: a joint is where bones meet; connective tissue links body parts
- **p014-i18** · answer_option · structure · both · exam high
  - b. Tendon
    - what: What is a tendon? → It is a strong fibrous connective-tissue structure that attaches muscle to bone and transmits pulling force. _(outside the book)_
    - where: What familiar body example shows this attachment? → The Achilles tendon connects the calf muscles to the heel bone. _(outside the book)_
      - how: How does a tendon help when someone stands on tiptoe? → Contracting calf muscles pull through the Achilles tendon on the heel bone, helping raise the heel. _(outside the book)_
  - **khola (unpack):**
    - Tendon yaani muscle का खिंचाव bone तक पहुँचाने वाली मजबूत डोरी जैसी बनावट · image: रस्सी खींचने पर उससे बँधा हिस्सा हिलना
    - kyon: Muscle के सिकुड़ने से bone तभी हिलेगी जब खिंचाव उस तक पहुँचे। _[standard]_ → Tendon muscle को bone से जोड़कर यह खिंचाव पहुँचाता है। _[standard]_
    - example / jagah: Achilles tendon behind the ankle, joining calf muscles to the heel bone.
    - aage: Damage to this connection can impair movement produced by the attached muscle.
    - screen par: pull and lift
    - is level ke liye: Use a three-part chain: muscle contracts → tendon transmits pull → bone moves.
  - prerequisites: muscle contraction creates a pull; bones act as moving supports
- **p014-i19** · answer_option · structure · both · exam high
  - c. Cartilage
    - what: What is cartilage? → It is a firm but flexible connective tissue providing support and, at many joints, a smooth load-bearing surface. _(outside the book)_
    - where: Where are familiar examples found? → It is found in the external ear and nose and over bone ends in many movable joints. _(outside the book)_
      - why: Why does cartilage at bone ends not make it the muscle attachment answer? → Articular cartilage helps distribute loads and reduce friction between moving bone surfaces; transmitting muscle pull to bone is the tendon's role. _(outside the book)_
  - **khola (unpack):**
    - Cartilage yaani कड़ा लेकिन कुछ लचीला connective tissue, जो सहारा देता है और कई joints में bone की सतह ढकता है · image: कान का ऊपरी हिस्सा दबने पर फिर आकार लेना
    - kyon: Joint में हिलती bone की सतहों पर भार पड़ता है और रगड़ हो सकती है। _[standard]_ → Cartilage चिकनी सहारा देने वाली सतह बनाता है; muscle की खींचने वाली डोरी tendon है। _[standard]_
    - example / jagah: External ear and articular surfaces of the knee.
    - aage: Location near a joint does not mean every connective tissue there has the same function.
    - screen par: cushion and glide
    - is level ke liye: Three-way distinction: tendon transmits pull, ligament stabilises, cartilage supports and smooths joint surfaces.
  - prerequisites: connective tissues have different functions; bones move against one another at joints
- **p014-i20** · answer_option · none · both · exam low
  - d. None
    - what: What would choosing 'None' assert here? → It would reject ligament, tendon and cartilage as the named muscle–bone connector.
      - why: Why should this option be rejected? → Tendon is already listed and correctly names the structure that attaches muscle to bone. _(outside the book)_
  - **khola (unpack):**
    - kyon: पहले देखना है कि दिए गए नामों में सही connection मौजूद है या नहीं। _[standard]_ → Tendon सही connection है, इसलिए 'None' नहीं चुनना है। _[standard]_
    - example / jagah: The tendon option immediately above.
    - aage: A verified positive match rules out 'None'.
    - screen par: match then eliminate
    - is level ke liye: Apply function matching before option elimination.
  - prerequisites: tendon attaches muscle to bone
